## Phase 1 — the ladder

Each rung is individually shippable, with one spec. **Core-first is a *re-ordering*, not a
replacement** — if that isn't explicit, Phase 1 becomes a six-month integration hole with no
green build.

> **A rung is COMPLETE only when it has NO open residuals.** A documented residual — a bug or gap the rung
> generated and did not close — means the rung is **NOT done**, however green its suite; its status says so
> (**◑**, not ✅). *"Core landed"* is not *"complete."* Residuals live with the rung (or its workstream) and
> are what keep it on this ladder until they close. Accepted debt measured linear-in-practice and a
> deliberate divergence are DECISIONS, not residuals — those do not hold a rung open.

| # | Mechanism | Note |
|---|---|---|
| **P1.0o** | **the compiler traces ITSELF — Workstream O1** ⭐ | **FIRST, because it is the instrument the rest of the ladder is debugged with.** shv2's stderr `Logger` dies the moment P1.0a interleaves N workers into one stream. A `__DebugStream` builtin in **`maxon.exe`** + 4 new event codes + a sink behind `Logger`'s existing API ⇒ binary events into the shared-memory ring, demuxed per-worker by `maxon monitor`. **Depends on NOTHING in Phase 1** — the bootstrap already carries the ring, the reserve, and the monitor *(see Workstream O)* |
| **P1.0a** | **grow the harness's parallel worker pool back** | **The acceptance target must exist before it can be a target.** Port `maxon-selfhosted`'s [`runAllSpecTestsParallel`](maxon-selfhosted/Testing/SpecTestRunner.maxon#L3401) worker pool into `maxon-shv2/Testing/`. Written in Maxon, compiled by **`maxon.exe`**, green under today's gates — so it lands *now*, and every later rung is measured against the real Phase-1 target instead of the serial stub. **Workstream S is what makes it pay:** the corpus takes the suite from 126 tests to thousands |
| **P1.0b** | **Workstream S — the `disabled-test:` marker, and ON-DEMAND porting** ⭐ | *(see Workstream S.)* The marker is SHIPPED (`362b07b72`). **The bulk port is NOT, and will not be** (user directive): spec files are copied from `/specs` **on demand, by the rung that needs them**, not as a corpus dump. A trial bulk sweep was run once, as a MEASUREMENT, and then discarded — see P1.0d, which is what it found |
| **P1.0d** | **complete the SCALAR CORE** ⭐⭐ | **NEW, and it exists because the sweep proved this plan's central claim false.** See "The scalar core is NOT done" below. ✅ **ALL SLICES DONE; P1.0d CLOSED 2026-07-15 — suite 126 → 355/0.** Slice 1 (parens · `true`/`false` · block scoping · void fns · top-level `typealias`) 126 → 159; then `.2` word/bitwise/chars, `.3` divide-by-zero, `.5a` top-level `let`, `.5b` globals, `.4` floats. |
| **P1.0d.2** | `not` / `and` / `or` · bitwise · character literals | ✅ **DONE.** Short-circuit `and`/`or` is control flow, so it landed as blocks + phis on the parser's on-the-fly SSA. Bitwise + chars are new `StdOp`s in the existing integer register class — **APPENDED at the END of a band** (a `match` range arm silently swallows anything inserted mid-band) |
| **P1.0d.3** | **`a / 0` ⇒ a clean panic** | ✅ **DONE 2026-07-15** — suite **279 → 281**. It escaped as a raw `0xC0000094` with **empty stderr**; it is now exit **1** + `panic: integer divide by zero` + a symbolized backtrace, and `specs/safety.md`'s `divide-by-zero` + `mod-by-zero` are ported and ENABLED. **Workstream R's first slice.** Three things this rung settled, each bigger than the divide: (1) **the fault is caught by a VEH thunk, not a divisor check** — shv2 is x64-only and the CPU raises `#DE` for free, so a `cmp`/`branch` before every `idiv` would be the scope cut the PRINCIPLE names; **NO gt redirect** (the reference's fault path rides green threads, which arrive at P1.5) — the thunk prints and exits in place, so the context travels as ordinary arguments and needs no fault globals at all. (2) ⭐ **FRAME POINTERS, on every function, leaves included** — see below. (3) the harness grew a ` ```stderr ` fence: `maxoncstderr` is the COMPILER's stderr, and a program's **RUNTIME** stderr was a thing no spec could pin |
| **P1.0d.5a** | **top-level `let`** ⚠ **the plan never listed this one either** | ✅ **DONE 2026-07-15** — suite **281 → 295**. **Found by probing, not by the ladder** — and it has **its own stable spec nobody had found: `specs/top-level-let.md`, 17 cases**, which the bootstrap passes. *(Missed twice because a grep for `global|static|module|init` does not match the filename — **look for a feature's own spec BY NAME.**)* **A module-scope constant is a NUMBER: it inlines as an ordinary `literal` at each use ⇒ no IR op, no `.data`, no relocation, NO BACKEND AT ALL.** Zero pre-existing goldens moved, which is the proof. Design: an arena of `(name, visibility, file, token range)` + a memoized DFS with cycle detection, driven inside `queryProgramSignatures` — that is what makes **forward references** and cross-file `export let` work. The evaluator **shares `TypeRules`' folds** (one opinion on constant arithmetic; only the climb loop forks, because `and`/`or` are *control flow* in a body and a *value* at file scope). ⚠ **Still `disabled-test:`: `basic-float-constant` (P1.0d.4), `file-private-same-name-cross-file` (P1.9 `as`), `from-literal-initializer` (P1.2 `String`)** |
| **P1.0d.5b** | **top-level `var` (GLOBALS)** ⚠ **another one the plan never listed** | ✅ **DONE 2026-07-15** — suite **295 → 317**. A module-scope global with a **real `.data` slot**. ⭐ **This rung created the Std MEMORY band** (`globalAddr` + `loadIndirect` + `storeIndirect`, v1's shape — NOT a fused `globalLoad`, because P1.1/P1.2 need the general pair) — **which begins retiring R1 @ P1.2's precondition** (see the R1 box: the Std-IR runtime route was blocked on the Std tier producing only `arith`/`call`). Plus `DataSectionEntry` in `GlobalDataTable`, a `.data` section in `PeWriter`, and the `dataSectionRipRelDisp32` arm. **NO `__module_init`** — initializers are constant-only, so they const-evaluate into `.data` **bytes**. ⚠ **`loadIndirect` MUST be `isPure: false`** or a global's read **would hoist out of a loop that writes it** — a silent wrong answer. ⚠ **CORRECTED 2026-07-15: this row said "pinned by a spec". IT IS NOT, AND NO SPEC CAN PIN IT — `StdOpMeta.isPure` has ZERO readers** (per-field sweep + a sabotage: flipped to `isPure: true`, the suite passes 371/0). The property holds because **nothing in the pipeline hoists**; the flag is a correct declaration awaiting DCE/CSE/LICM/the inliner. The spec pins the ANSWER, not the flag. **See OPEN.md #27.** ⭐ **shv2 does NOT inherit the bootstrap's aliasing bug (OPEN.md #17):** identity is per-FILE **by mechanism** (`fileScopedDeclKey(name, readerFilePath)`), label = bare name + `$1` **only on collision** (path-free, so goldens stay stable; `$` is structurally unwritable — `isAlphaNum` is `[A-Za-z0-9_]`). **The corpus had no `var` twin of `file-private-same-name-cross-file`, so we wrote one**: shv2 returns **118**, the bootstrap **212**. ✅ **`short-circuit-elision.md` RETIRED** — its 5 divide-by-zero workaround cases are superseded by the real corpus cases; its 9 genuine *lowering* tests moved to `short-circuit-lowering.md` (goldens are **R100 renames** — proof the codegen did not move). ✅ **`RequiredData` blocks now actually compare** (they were **silently ignored**) |
| **P1.0d.4** | **floats (f64)** | ✅ **DONE 2026-07-15 — suite 317/19 → 355/0, and P1.0d IS CLOSED.** Wave 1 (an all-caller-saved XMM pool; a float across a call force-spills). ⭐ **ONE `X64Register` enum, xmm0-15 at rawValue 16-31 — the low nibble IS the encoding number, so `and 0xF` encodes and bit 4 classifies.** The design that paid for itself: **CLASS-AGNOSTIC OPS, CLASS-DISPATCHED ENCODERS** — a move/spill/reload/swap is a class-agnostic *concept*, so there is no float spill op, no float move, no float `xchg`; the encoder picks `mov` vs `movsd` from `regClassOf`. ⇒ `SsaDestruction` needs **no float case**, the splitter no float spill op, coloring is class-blind, and a spill slot is 8 bytes = an f64. *(v1 mints a SECOND enum and pays a parallel op per emit, threading a bare `regClass` int through each — that tax is absent here.)* **The class travels FORWARD** (`ValueClassColumns`, per FUNCTION — ValueIds are function-local — produced by `StdToX64Conversion`, consumed by the allocator; the Target tier carries no types, so it *cannot* derive one). ⚠ **`operandType` is the SOURCE and the OPCODE names the result** — true only for `siToFp`/`fpToSi`, the dialect's only cross-class ops. `cmp` is ALWAYS `i1` (a float compare's answer is a GPR bool). Float `/` is `StdBinOpcode.div` on `binOp`, **not** `StdOp.div`: `idiv` faults and `divsd` does not, and purity is the band's membership rule. ⭐ **`<`/`<=` SWAP OPERANDS** into the already-NaN-correct `ja`/`jae` family (`ucomisd` sets CF on unordered) — one jump, four of six predicates shape-identical to an integer compare; only `==`/`!=` need parity, **as a second BLOCK**, so `IrBlock.CondBranch`'s one-branch-per-block invariant is never bent and v1's phi-copy miscompile is **unrepresentable, not fixed** (`float-compare-branch.md`, 11/11). **`E3009`, not a new code**, for narrowing — see the ⚠ row below |
| | ⚠ **What P1.0d.4 cost, and it is the ledger** | **The contract was WRONG TEN TIMES**, and every one shares a shape: **TYPE surfaces specified without OPERATIONS** — `operandType` with no ops consuming it, a register enum with no instructions, a float type with no conversions (**`trunc` had 0 hits in shv2**). **Nine were ONE FACT WRITTEN DOWN TWICE**, the file's own through-line: `classPoolSize` returned the FILE's width (16) where the POOL's (14) was meant *with the correct number in the comment directly above it*; `globalStdType` kept a second tag→StdType table that had diverged (no `float` arm); `notOperatorFor`'s range arm swallowed `float` **while its header boasted the `match` had FIXED that exact fall-through**; `floatCondCodeForPred` + `floatCmpSwapsOperands` had to agree with nothing making them; `valueConfinedTo` and `analyzePressure` gave opposite answers about a ∅-allowed value; the bootstrap **disagreed with itself** (`CoerceValueToExpectedKind` rejected float→int, `ConvertArgToParamType` truncated it) — **and the fix for THAT re-spelled the integer family 19 lines from its home**, which the review caught. ⭐⭐ **`Parser.mintPhi` hardcoded `argType: i64`, and its comment NAMED THIS RUNG**: *"the scaffold the float (XMM) register class will need at P1.0d.4, and that is when it acquires a consumer and a reason to be right."* **The rung gave it the consumer and not the reason** ⇒ `var f = 0.0` in a loop — the commonest float idiom there is — **crashed the emitter**, and the authored spec could not see it (its phis carry INTs). **Only extending the instrument found it.** ⇒ **READ THE COMMENT THAT NAMES YOUR RUNG** |
| | ⚠ **Deferred out of P1.0d.4, named not hidden** | ✅ **Float NAMEABLE (F1) LANDED 2026-07-19** (`fda8f73bb`, 756→761): `typealias F = float(low to high)` now parses + resolves to `MaxonType.float`, enabling the 5 `ranged-typealias.md` float-LOCAL tests (float-range, f32-range/arithmetic/comparison/to-int — float stays local via the no-op `as`-cast, `trunc`→int, no ABI). ✅✅ **THE FLOAT FEATURE IS COMPLETE 2026-07-19 ("do BOTH": F1 nameable → F2a return → F2b args).** F2a (float RETURN #50, `f8c516e33`): float-alias return types resolve to float + XMM0/d0 return codegen → `float-return-from-function` GREEN. F2b (float ARGS #57, `c428191ac`, 762→763): float param/arg in XMM/d-reg by a SEPARATE int/FP counter (v1's convention) + `buildCalleeParamClasses` + `emitCallArgMoves` → `f32-function-param-return` GREEN. ⭐ F2b review caught a real ARM64 compiler crash (shared liveness decoded float-arg regs through an x64-only door — the signature bug) — fixed x64-byte-identical. ✅ **The float NUMBER-TYPE story landed 2026-07-19**: F1 nameable → F2a return (#50) → F2b args (#57) → F3a int→float widening (`cc698eaf9`) → F3b throwing-float return (#60, `0b113576a`), suite 767/0 — nameable typealias, return, args, int→float coercion, and the throwing-return throw edge, on x64 + arm64 (cross-host golden-synced). ◑ **But floats are NOT COMPLETE — three follow-ups remain (below): `f64→f32` #19, `floor`/`ceil`/`round`, and float const-folding.** — **Wave 2 = the float ABI** (a float PARAM/RETURN needs the xmm arg slots; **measured** — `takeFloat(42.0)`, a literal with no coercion, panics in the callee's ABI reception). Win64 preserves all **128 bits** of xmm6-15 and `movsd` ZEROES 64-127, so callee-saved XMMs drag in 16-byte slots + leaf misalignment together — that is why Wave 1 is all-caller-saved. Also: **`floor`/`ceil`/`round`** (`roundsd` is SSE4.1, a three-byte escape) ⇒ their own rung; **float const-FOLDING** is rejected (E2015) — it needs software IEEE-754 in the compiler, and v1 does not do it either; **`0.0 - x` ≠ true negation on `-(+0.0)`** — unobservable until `print` (P1.2), and v1 has shipped it for its whole life; **`f64→f32` still coerces implicitly (#19)** — the same lossy-narrowing bug wearing different types, outside the float→int ruling, **unresolved** (its own lossy-narrowing rung — spec + both compilers); **`trunc` is reserved** wherever a call is parsed, silently shadowing a user function (v1 has the identical property for its whole intrinsic set) ⇒ ✅✅ **ALL THREE FOLLOW-UPS CLOSED 2026-07-22 (P1.0d.4 COMPLETE, 767→1313):** #19 was NOT "unresolved outside the float→int ruling" — the oracle settles it by RUNNING (compile-time E3005 for a literal, a runtime panic guard for a non-literal, the SAME rule P1.9 gave ints; shv2 just never built the float arm) → **slice B**. `floor`/`ceil`/`round`/`sqrt` → **slice C** (SSE4.1 `roundsd`, ties-to-even). And **float const-folding did NOT "need software IEEE-754"** — that claim was wrong at the code: the real blocker was that shv2 had no `i64↔f64` reinterpret; **slice D1** added `__Builtins.bitsToFloat` to the bootstrap (the mirror of the existing `floatToBits`), and **slice D2** folds via `bitsToFloat`→host-f64→`floatToBits`, borrowing the host's IEEE arithmetic rather than re-implementing it (a 2nd copy of what `parseFloatBits` already encodes). `-0.0` is now sign-correct (A′'s `parseNegatedNumericLiteral`). |
| **P1.0c** | **measure the stdlib cone** | against the **upgraded** harness. Cheap, and it sets the boundary — see above |

### ⚠⚠ "The scalar core is DONE" was FALSE — measured 2026-07-13

The 126 spec tests shv2 passes were all written **by shv2, for shv2**. Run against `/specs` — the real,
accumulated definition of the language, written by people who were not trying to make shv2 look good —
the scalar core scored **48 passing out of 2,746**. *Not one of the 126 self-authored tests had ever
used a parenthesis.* **This is exactly the bias Workstream S exists to break, and it broke it on the
first run.** Every item below was reproduced against the compiler, not inferred:

| Gap | Evidence |
|---|---|
| **parenthesized expressions** | `(a + b) * c` ⇒ `E2004: Expected expression but got '('`. The Pratt parser has **no grouping primary** |
| **`true` / `false` literals** | ⇒ `E2004: Expected expression but got 'true'`. The `bool` TYPE exists (comparisons make it, `if` eats it); only the literals are missing |
| **`not` / `and` / `or`** | not parseable |
| **top-level `typealias`** | ⇒ `E2015: Unsupported: top-level typealias (M1 parses only function declarations)`. **1080 cases** — the single biggest blocker in the corpus, and the plan had it scheduled LAST, at P1.9. Most `/specs` files simply OPEN with `typealias Integer = int(i64.min to i64.max)` |
| **block scoping** | `Parser.maxon` calls **neither** `Scope.pushScope` nor `popScope` — both are correct, and both are **DEAD CODE** (`Scope.maxon:267,276`). Every `if`/`while` body declares into the FUNCTION frame. One twin silently accepts an invalid program; the other feeds malformed SSA to the allocator and panics (`RegisterAllocator.maxon:645` — a **symptom**, not the cause) |
| **void functions** | **ANY** void function panics the compiler, *even if never called* (`IrBlock.maxon:238`, no terminator). A second, distinct panic fires on an explicit bare `return` (`LowerMaxonToStd.maxon:352`), whose own message wrongly assumes only `main` can be void |
| **`a / 0`** | escapes as a raw `0xC0000094` hardware trap. `specs/safety.md` requires a clean `panic` + exit 1 |
| **floats · chars · bitwise** | had **no rung anywhere on the ladder**. Folded into P1.0d (user decision): they are scalar primitives, not mechanisms. ✅ **ALL THREE DONE** — bitwise + chars at P1.0d.2, floats at P1.0d.4 |

**✅ EVERY ROW ABOVE IS NOW CLOSED. P1.0d IS DONE, and the SCALAR CORE IS — this time measured, not asserted: 355/355, from 48-of-2,746 against `/specs` when the claim was first made.** The table above is kept because the lesson outlived it: *"the scalar core is DONE"* was **false**, and it was false because the 126 tests it rested on were **written by shv2, for shv2** — not one had ever used a parenthesis. **The corpus is the only judge that was not trying to make shv2 look good.**

⚠ **AND THAT IS STILL TRUE OF THE THING YOU ARE ABOUT TO SHIP.** P1.0d.4 shipped **float negation** and **float phis** with **ZERO test coverage** — the first was caught only by porting `unary-negation.md`, the second only by teaching `scale-test` to emit a float. Both were features the rung *itself wrote*. ⇒ **Step 0 of a rung is the SPEC PORT LIST, and it is the coordinator's** (now in the rung skill, `03b4a47dc`). Inheriting a ported corpus is not doing it.

### ⭐ FRAME POINTERS, on every function — decided at P1.0d.3, but the forcing reason is **P1.5**

**shv2 now emits `push rbp` / `mov rbp, rsp` in EVERY prologue, leaves included** (user decision,
2026-07-15). It previously used frame-pointer-omitted (rsp-only) frames **while still reserving rbp** —
a **strictly dominated** state: it paid the register cost and took none of the benefit. **rbp stays
NON-allocatable; the pool is unchanged at 14.** Addressing stays **rsp-relative** — the divergence from
v1 is deliberate and narrow: **rbp is for the CHAIN, not for addressing.**

**The stack trace only EXPOSED this. The reason it is not a P1.0d.3 detail is
[`__gt_morestack`](maxon-selfhosted/Compiler/Targets/X64/X64Backend.maxon#L7094):** v1 grows a green-thread
stack by allocating 2×, copying, and then **FIXING THE SAVED-RBP CHAIN by the relocation offset** (step 6b
walks the chain on the new stack, adjusting every saved rbp in the old range). GT stacks are 2 KB initial
and **RELOCATE** on growth, Go-`runtime.morestack`-style. ⇒ **A relocating stack must find and fix every
interior frame pointer, and the chain is how it finds them.** **R3 @ P1.5 commits shv2 to exactly that
model**, so shv2 needs frame pointers there *regardless of panics*. Landing them now moved all 279 goldens
**once**; retrofitting at P1.5 moves the same goldens **then**, onto a shipped runtime. *Do the hard things
early.*

⚠ **A frameless leaf could not be spared:** you cannot unwind OUT of one without knowing its frame layout,
and the faulting `main` **is** one — so framing only "framed" functions does not help. (And because no shv2
function ever wrote rbp, rbp was *invariant program-wide*: walking `[rbp]` gave garbage, not a short trace.)
**`.pdata`/`.xdata` + `RtlVirtualUnwind` is the FPO-correct alternative and was rejected: NO compiler in
this tree emits unwind info**, so it has no port source and is its own rung — and it does **not** solve
morestack's fixup, which needs the chain anyway.

⇒ **P1.0d lands before P1.1**, and it pulls the **declaration half of P1.9 forward** (parse + resolve a
top-level ranged `typealias`; the corpus's ranges are wide, so the *checks* — `ExpandCastRangeChecks` /
`InsertRangeChecks` — stay at P1.9 where they belong). Floats need a whole new register bank (there is
**no XMM class** in the allocator today), so they are the last slice of the rung, not the first.

Promoted ahead of structs 2026-07-15 — see the RESTRUCTURED box: a struct is a heap value, so the heap cannot come second.)* The slab allocator · `__mm_alloc` · `__mm_incref`/`__mm_decref` · the `__destruct_*` cascade. **The allocator ALWAYS RETURNS ZEROED MEMORY, from commit 1** — a property of the allocator, not of each caller (it cost v1 three separately root-caused bugs; see ARCHITECTURE.md → "Allocator: the zeroing contract"). ⚠ **THIS RUNG MUST DECIDE THE RUNTIME'S *FORM*** — hand-assembled machine code vs. v1's `runtime.std` (6,049 lines of Std-IR text through the ordinary backend). **The excuse for deferring it has now EXPIRED: its precondition was the Std memory band, and P1.0d.5b shipped it.** See the R1 box in Workstream R — **the named risk is inertia**, 6 hand-assembled functions becoming 5,000 by default. **Write the reason down.** ⚠ Its acceptance test is **P1.1a**, not a bespoke spec: an allocator with no heap value to allocate is untestable, so the two are adjacent on purpose |

> ### ⭐ P1.2 — two decisions taken elsewhere that land HERE (2026-07-15)
>
> **1. `String.toByteArray()` is a MOVE, and the source is not mutable afterwards** (user decision).
> This is an OWNERSHIP statement, which is why it belongs to this rung and not to the bootstrap's
> refcounting. It **dissolves** a problem rather than managing it: with one owner there is no second
> observer to keep consistent, so there is no aliasing question, **no copy-on-write**, and no
> "independent value" contract to enforce.
>
> The bootstrap cannot do this — it has no ownership — so it takes the fallback: `toByteArray()` returns
> an **independent** ByteArray, implemented as a COW view (`185351d1f`). **That is a stopgap for a
> compiler without moves, not a design to port.** Two of its specs
> (`specs/string-type-2` / `tobytearray-is-independent-of-an-owned-source`,
> `tobytearray-survives-the-source-growing`) **use the string AFTER `toByteArray()` and must become
> use-after-move ERRORS here.** Delete them at this rung with that reason, do not make them pass.
>
> The bootstrap's COW is also **one-directional** — a write through the source's raw `managed` shows
> through a view that has not detached yet (measured). Moves make that unrepresentable. **Do not port
> the COW view, and do not port the hole.**
>
> **2. ⚠ v1's two-result-capture MISCOMPILE is waiting for THIS allocator.** shv2's register allocator is
> a deliberately different **linear SSA-chordal** design — i.e. v1's shape, where virtuals are colored
> independently and call-result **capture movs are real**. v1 paid for this bug already
> ([CopyResolution.maxon:377-395](maxon-selfhosted/Compiler/Targets/Shared/CopyResolution.maxon#L377-L395)):
> the colorer can legitimately assign two result dests into a **SWAP** or **CHAIN**, which naive
> sequential movs cannot realize — `mov x1, x0; mov x0, x1` collapsed value+flag into one register, so a
> bounds check read the element value instead of the OOB flag and **panicked on in-bounds data** (the
> array-sort `try get(j) otherwise` miscompile). The fix is `sequentializeCallResultCapture`: a
> **parallel-copy sequencer with per-class cycle-break scratch**, never sequential movs.
>
> The **bootstrap is immune and its code is therefore NOT the model** — its `Assign(reg, value)` merely
> pins a value into a register map and emits no capture mov at all, so it has nothing to sequence.
> **Port v1's lesson here, not the bootstrap's shortcut.**

### 🚩 PHASE 1 GATE — the differential oracle

**Acceptance — and step 1 is the whole point:**
1. Compile the **parallel** harness with **`maxon.exe`** → `spec-runner-ref.exe`.
2. Compile the **parallel** harness with **`maxon-shv2.exe`** → `spec-runner-shv2.exe`.
3. Run **both** against `specs-shv2/`, both driving `maxon-shv2.exe` as the compiler-under-test,
   **both on an N-worker pool**.
4. **The two must produce identical results.**

If shv2 miscompiles the harness, the shv2-built harness's own verdicts are untrustworthy — so the
`maxon.exe`-built harness is the oracle that catches it. **Without step 1 this gate could pass
while silently broken.**

**Additional gates the parallel pool brings — and they are a feature, not a tax:**
- **Worker-count invariance:** `-j1` and `-jN` must produce **identical results**. This is the
  same shape as the 1-core-vs-N-core byte-identity gate the plan already demands of P2.6's
  fan-out, and it is the sharpest test of `async` correctness that exists — a dropped or
  double-freed capture shows up as a flake, and a flake is a bug.
- **`mm-trace` must stay clean under the pool.** A green-thread capture that leaks or
  double-decrefs is exactly what P1.5 exists to get right, and this is where it is proven.

**Then the circle closes:** the shv2-compiled, shv2-parallel harness runs the spec suite that
tests shv2 — which is what `maxon-selfhosted` does, and is the Phase-1 goal.

### Future rungs — corpus-surfaced features that each need their own number

Distinct front-end / IR features the corpus surfaced that do NOT ride an existing rung; each gets a ladder
number when it is sequenced (each also has a `disabled-test:` or oracle-divergence pinning it):

- **⭐⭐ USER RULING 2026-07-25 — ADOPT GO'S STRUCTURE FOR BOTH GT-STACK ENTRIES BELOW ("we should just copy what Go does").** Applies to *this* entry (per-M system stack) and the fault-headroom entry after it. **COPY THE STRUCTURE:** (1) **a per-M system stack**, and specifically **g0 = the M's OWN OS THREAD STACK**, not a synthesized region — Go allocates nothing for it. That is **cheaper than what EITHER Maxon compiler does today** (the bootstrap `VirtualAlloc`s a separate 64 KB per P, `X86CodeEmitter.Runtime.cs:4355-4365`; shv2 commits one global 64 KB): per-M by construction, zero extra allocation, ~1 MB demand-paged rather than a fixed 64 KB, and — the real prize — **NO TIB `StackBase`/`StackLimit` REPOINTING**, because the thread's own bounds are already correct for its own stack. shv2 has already had to de-duplicate that repoint idiom once (`emitRepointTibToSystemStack`, shared between shim and morestack); this DELETES it instead of sharing it. (2) **POSIX: `sigaltstack` + `SA_ONSTACK`** (the bootstrap already does this for its FAULT handler and merely omits it on the debug TRAP handler). (3) **Windows fault headroom reserved INSIDE the stack**, counted in BOTH the allocation and the guard, à la `_StackSystem`. ⚠ **The coordinator's earlier "red zone mapped BELOW the stack base" proposal is WITHDRAWN and Go's in-stack placement is better FOR SHV2 SPECIFICALLY**: shv2's morestack **copies and relocates** stacks, so a below-base red zone would have to be re-established on every relocation — a fresh invariant to maintain, i.e. exactly the class of thing that rots; reserving inside means `morestack` maintains it for free. **⚠ DO NOT COPY THE NUMBERS.** `2048` / `928` / `512*PtrSize` are Go's frame sizes and Go's platform assumptions; **Maxon has now twice copied Go's constants without Go's structure** (this entry and the next), and taking the constants again without measuring would be the same error mirrored. **Copy the structure, MEASURE the sizes against Maxon's own frames.** The rationale is not authority-by-Google: Go's structure makes the invariant *unrepresentable* rather than *documented*, which is the precise property both entries below are missing.
- **🔴 B1b (MULTI-M) PREREQUISITE, TODAY UNRECORDED: THE SYSTEM STACK IS ONE GLOBAL REGION, NOT PER-M — AND ITS SAFETY INVARIANT IS PROSE ONLY.** `GtSystemStackSize = 0x10000` is allocated **once** in `__gt_init` with its top published to a single `.data` global `__gt_system_stack_top` (`GtRuntime.maxon:203-208,787-798`), and the syscall shim sets `rsp` to that **fixed** top on **every** entry (`X64GtRuntime.maxon:288,321`). The invariant that makes this safe is written down in exactly one place, as a comment (`X64GtRuntime.maxon:214-216`): *"⚠ NO NESTING … Safe only because a kernel call is a LEAF that never re-enters guarded Maxon code, **and single-M runs one thing at a time** — a shimmed call must never reach another shimmed call on the same thread."* **Leg 2 of that invariant is falsified by B1b BY DEFINITION.** Two Ms inside shimmed calls concurrently both load the same top and write the same 64 KB region ⇒ **silent interleaved corruption, not a clean fault** — the worst failure shape. **Nothing enforces it:** no assertion, no gate, and **none of this file's six B1b mentions names the system stack**, while B1b is repeatedly filed *"off the critical path"* — so the fact and the change that will break it do not reference each other. On the DERIVE > CHECK > COMMENT ladder this sits at the weakest rung, guarding a silent-corruption failure. **⭐ BOTH REFERENCES SOLVE IT STRUCTURALLY, SO THE INVARIANT CANNOT BE VIOLATED RATHER THAN MERELY BEING FORBIDDEN:** Go gives **each M its own g0 system stack** — `systemstack()` switches to the *current* M's g0, and on Windows `asmstdcall` runs Win32 calls there — so no global exists to contend over; and **the C# bootstrap does the same**, where `PSystemStackSize` (`GtLayout.cs:178`) is a **per-P** constant sitting beside `POffSystemStackSP = 0x70` (*"saved OS-thread RSP for syscalls/morestack"*). **shv2 copied the SIZE from that per-P constant and made the REGION global** — the number came across, the structure did not, exactly the shape of the `_StackSystem` omission in the entry below. **⇒ FIX WHEN B1b IS SEQUENCED: a per-P system stack (the bootstrap's shape, Go's shape), which DELETES the invariant instead of documenting it.** ⚠ Re-check **leg 1** at the same time: *"a kernel call is a LEAF that never re-enters guarded Maxon code"* also breaks if any Win32 call re-enters Maxon — an IOCP completion routine, APC, or callback — and subprocess/stdio (workstream C) is precisely where that arises. Not a live defect today (shv2 is single-M and the shim's callers are leaves); filed so the rung that falsifies it finds it.
- **🔴 A CPU FAULT INSIDE AN `async` FUNCTION LOSES ITS PANIC ENTIRELY AND DIES `0xC0000005` (x64-windows). MEASURED by the coordinator 2026-07-25, not inferred.** Identical program, identical fault, only the STACK differs: on the main thread `let zero = 0; 100 / zero` gives `panic: integer divide by zero` + a symbolized trace (`in boom / in main / in mrt_start`), **exit 1** — exactly what `specs-shv2/safety.md`'s `divide-by-zero` test asserts. Wrap the same `boom()` in `let p = async boom(); await p` and stderr is **EMPTY** and the exit is **3221225477 = `0xC0000005` STATUS_ACCESS_VIOLATION**. ⇒ **A SPECIFIED, TESTED behaviour silently breaks when the same code runs on a green thread, and no test in the suite covers that combination.** **MECHANISM:** Windows delivers an exception on the *current thread's* stack — `KiUserExceptionDispatcher` writes `CONTEXT` (1232 B) + `EXCEPTION_RECORD` (152 B) + machine frame, **~1.5 KB below RSP, BEFORE any VEH runs**. A GT stack is `GtStackSize` = **0x800 (2 KB)** with `GtStackGuardMargin` = **0x3A0 (928 B)** of slack (`GtRuntime.maxon:191,200`), so the kernel's write runs off the end into unmapped memory and the process is gone before `X64Runtime`'s fault thunk — the thing that *prints* the panic — can execute. **⚠ THE 64 KB SYSTEM STACK DOES NOT AND CANNOT COVER THIS**, and that is the crux: `__gt_system_stack_top` solves **VOLUNTARY** kernel entry (the syscall shim picks the moment and switches RSP before a Win32 call); a fault is **INVOLUNTARY** — the CPU traps at an arbitrary instruction and there is no hook to switch stacks in first. shv2 has the system stack *and still dies*. **GO HIT THIS AND SOLVES IT TWICE:** on POSIX a per-M `sigaltstack` + `SA_ONSTACK` (the kernel switches stacks, so the small goroutine stack is never used); on Windows, where VEH has no altstack, it RESERVES the space via `_StackSystem` (`512*PtrSize` = **4 KB** on amd64) counted **TWICE** — in the allocation (`_FixedStack0 = _StackMin + _StackSystem`) *and* in the guard (`_StackGuard = 928*mult + _StackSystem`) so morestack fires while the reserve is intact. **shv2 carried over Go's `_StackMin` (2048) and the `928`, but NOT `_StackSystem`** — the one term that exists for exactly this — the same omission the C# bootstrap made (`GtLayout.cs`; see `docs/DEBUGGER_DESIGN.md` **P4d-GT-STACK**, where it was found as *"a breakpoint on a green-thread stack kills the debuggee"*). **CANDIDATE FIX, CHEAPER THAN GO'S — a PROPOSAL for the rung to validate, NOT a measured design:** map a small red zone below each GT stack sized for the kernel's pre-handler write (~1.5 KB ⇒ one page) so that write lands in MAPPED memory, and have the fault thunk switch RSP to `__gt_system_stack_top` as its FIRST action and run the handler body there. Go must fold a full 4 KB into every goroutine stack because it has no pre-existing system stack to hand off to; shv2 does, so it may pay one page per GT instead of inflating every stack — the system stack earning its keep twice. Whatever is chosen, the footprint is a **global per-GT memory tradeoff and must be MEASURED, not asserted**. ⚠ **shv2's exposure is STRICTLY LARGER than the bootstrap's**: the bootstrap forbids unguarded division statically (`E3103` when foldable, else `E3057` demanding `try/otherwise` or a non-zero ranged type), so safe bootstrap code cannot even reach a `#DE`; shv2 has no such check yet and `safety.md` deliberately specifies a *runtime* panic — so ordinary safe shv2 code reaches it today. **Acceptance when sequenced:** the `async` twin of `safety.md`'s divide-by-zero panics with a correct symbolized trace and exit 1; plus the arm64 question answered (POSIX `sigaltstack` is the right shape there, and the bootstrap already does it for its fault handler but NOT its debug trap handler).

- **⚠⚠ `regalloc:splitting` IS QUADRATIC WHEN A FLOAT IS LIVE ACROSS CALLS — MEASURED ×4.24 PER DOUBLING, PRE-EXISTING, AND STRUCTURALLY INVISIBLE TO `scale-test`.** Found 2026-07-25 by the 2b-iii optimizer; **filed as a RUNG, deliberately NOT as Workstream-O "measured debt", because it is measured QUADRATIC rather than linear-in-practice.** Same generated program, changing only two locals' declared type: **float** 1,561,997 → 6,628,499 → 28,064,380 bytes at N=128/256/512 (**×4.24 ×4.23**) against **int** 1,762 → 2,660 → 4,454 (×1.51 ×1.67) — **6,300× at N=512**. Not the known liveness-recompute debt: `regalloc:liveness` beside it is ×1.86/×1.92, linear. Emitted code grows linearly (×1.84/×1.91), so the split COUNT is linear and **the per-split repair is proportional to the whole function** — the victim is live across the entire body, so its dirty region IS the body. **ROOT CAUSE, and the source already names it: x64 has NO callee-saved XMM** (`Targets/X64/X64Backend.maxon:653` — *"the fact that makes taking xmm6-15 as callee-saved a Wave 2 problem rather than a free win"*), so a float live across a call is forbidden **every** XMM and every call forces a store/reload bracket. **PRE-EXISTING, PROVEN**: the base binary reproduces the identical curve (float control 28,598,570 base vs 28,598,582 head at N=512 — the same flat +12 the rung costs), and the control program contains no conformance dispatch at all. ⚠ **`scale-test` CANNOT see it** — splitting on the corpus reads ×1.71/1.85/1.93/1.98/2.00, because the corpus holds no float across a call. **WHY IT IS A RUNG AND NOT AN OPTIMIZER EDIT:** the structural cure is making xmm6–15 callee-saved, which **changes codegen for every float program** — so it needs its own contract, its own golden churn and its own justification, and it violates the optimization pass's non-negotiable byte-identical-Target-IR gate by construction. **RE-MEASURE TRIGGER:** any float held across a call in a hot function; a corpus knob that generates one would make it visible to the ladder for the first time.
- **⚠ A `bool` CLOSURE PARAMETER TRAPS ON WASM — `wasm trap: indirect call type mismatch` (exit 3).** Found 2026-07-25 by the 2b-iii review while probing bool routes; **PRE-EXISTING** (reproduces on the pre-rung compiler) and **not this rung's**. Minimal repro: `let pass = function(x bool) gives x` then `pass(true)` — x64 exit **0**, wasm exit **3**. An `int` closure parameter is fine; a **named** function value with a `bool` param is fine. **ROOT CAUSE, both halves read:** `Targets/Wasm/StdToWasm.maxon:472` (`indirectCallParamValTypes`) declares every non-float indirect-call param **i64**, and `Runtime/FnRefThunk.maxon:200` (`thunkUserParamStdType`) normalizes every non-float param to `StdType.i64` — which is exactly why a **thunked** function value matches. A **LIFTED CLOSURE is not thunked**: it declares its source param types verbatim, so a `bool` param is `StdType.i1` → `wasmValTypeOf` → **i32**. The funcref's real type is `(i32, i64) → i64` while the `call_indirect` immediate says `(i64, i64) → i64`. ⭐ **The stated defect: the param model is TWO-valued (float / not-float) where the truth is THREE-valued (f64 / i32 / i64).** The suite is green over it only because no wasm-enabled closure spec uses a `bool` parameter. **Belongs to the P1.5-A1/A2 function-value workstream**, not to conformance.
- **⚠ FLOAT AS A GENERIC TYPE ARGUMENT PANICS THE BACKEND — `X64Backend.maxon:703`, xmm0 → rcx crosses register files.** Filed 2026-07-25; **PRE-EXISTING and coordinator-reproduced on the base**: `type Box uses T` + `typealias FBox = Box with float` + `FBox.create(2.5)`, **with no `where` clause at all**, panics. A float in an opaque `T` slot has no float-aware marshalling. ⚠ **2b-iii WIDENS THE DOOR, and the filed scope must say so:** admitting `float` to `Comparable`/`Equatable` means `Pair with Decimal where T is Comparable` — which was a clean **E3017** on base — now reaches that same panic, and **the constrained spelling is the one a user is most likely to write**. Neither form miscompiles (both fail the build) and the panic names the real gap, which is why it did not hold the rung. **The cure IS this rung's deliverable**: float-aware marshalling for an opaque `T`, plus a positioned diagnostic for the shapes that remain unsupported — which would land on `Box with float` too. Adjacent to the Std-tier bitcast that `float.hash` needs.
- **⚠ A NUMERIC-LITERAL METHOD RECEIVER MIS-LEXES — `7.hash()` and `1.5.equals(1.5)`.** Filed 2026-07-25; **PRE-EXISTING, and BOTH compilers reject it** (shv2: *"'if' requires a bool condition, got 'float'"*; the bootstrap: `E2010 Expected characterliteral`). `Lexer.maxon:972-1030`'s number scanner treats a `.` after digits as a decimal point unconditionally and then swallows a SECOND dot into the literal. Equally broken for `int` since the direct-dispatch rung, **no canonical corpus case exercises it**, and it is a clean compile error rather than a miscompile — so it is a lexer rung, not a conformance one. Bindings are unaffected (`let a = 1.5; a.equals(1.5)` compiles and runs).
- **⚠ A USER `type String` SHADOWS THE SYNTHESIZED `String.hash`/`String.equals` CONFORMER, AND IT COMPILES — LAST-WINS, NOT A DIAGNOSTIC.** Filed 2026-07-25 by the coordinator; surfaced (and MEASURED) by the direct-primitive-dispatch rung, but **pre-existing on the WITNESS path since Slice C**. `TypeResolution.isCompilerOwnedTypeName` (`:189`) refuses a user declaration that mints a nominal identity under `ExitCode`/`HashValue`/`Ordering` (2b-ii, E2015) — **but NOT under `String`.** So a user `type String` with its own `hash()` legally mints the symbol `String.hash`, which is the exact name `BuiltinConformanceRuntime` installs its synthesized djb2 body under (`qualifiedMethodName(StringConformerName, …)`). A program with a user `type String` AND a `Box with String where T is Hashable` therefore puts **two functions named `String.hash` in `module.functions`**, and `indexFunctionsByName` **upserts last-wins** — no duplicate check, no diagnostic. ⭐ **MEASURED, not inferred:** in the dispatch rung's intermediate state a user `type String` whose `hash()` returns 7 returned **5381 — djb2's SEED constant** — because the synthesized body had been installed over the user's function. That rung cured its own *install* side (candidates narrowed to symbols the module does not already define), so the collision is currently masked rather than fixed. ⭐⭐ **AND IT IS A REACHABLE `0xC0000005`, NOT MERELY A WRONG ANSWER — measured by the dispatch rung's independent review, and ATTRIBUTED to the base by building `874e2f7bd` itself**: a user `type String` **plus** `Box with String where T is Hashable` FAULTS at runtime, **with or without a user `hash()` body**, identically on base. Root cause sits UPSTREAM of the install: `isIntrinsicBuiltinConformance` keys on the NAME `"String"`, so a user struct of that name is believed to conform intrinsically and is then handled as the builtin managed String RECORD — a struct pointer read through a fused-record layout it does not have. ⭐ **The proof that the synthesized body was silently replacing the user's**: at base both variants emit **2771 bytes, byte-identical whether or not the user wrote a `hash()`**; after the dispatch rung they differ (2695 vs 2771), i.e. the user's body is now the one that survives. ⇒ The `undefinedImplNames` narrowing is sound and this rung IMPROVES the case; **the name collision underneath remains, and it is a live segfault on `main` today.** ⚠ **`int` is structurally immune and that is the whole asymmetry**: `int` is a KEYWORD, so no user symbol can ever wear the `int.` qualifier — which is exactly why the dispatch rung scoped its widened `isCompilerInternalCallee` to `int.` only and deliberately left `String.*` out. **What the rung owes — this is a DESIGN CHOICE, not a bug fix, and the three options diverge:** (a) add `String` to `isCompilerOwnedTypeName`, refusing a user `type String` — symmetric with `Ordering`, but a sharper divergence from the bootstrap, which permits it because its stdlib is NAMESPACED (`stdlib.String.hash`) and shv2 has no namespacing; (b) give the synthesized conformer a distinguishable symbol — but it must stay nameable by a `funcAbs64` witness reloc, which is why it is not `__`-prefixed today; (c) keep both symbols legal and make the collision a DIAGNOSTIC rather than a silent last-wins. ⇒ Whichever is chosen, **the silent last-wins must not survive**: it is a wrong answer that no committed test reaches. **RELATED ASYMMETRY, accepted for now and recorded so a reader does not trip over it:** an `ExitCode`-tagged receiver does not dispatch — `ExitCode` is `int(0 to u32.max)` but carries its OWN value tag, and `canonicalizeRangedAlias`/`rangedAliasTag` reduce only `named` types, so `let a as ExitCode = 3` then `a.compare(b)` falls to the pre-existing clean E2015 while `Integer`/`Byte`/bare `int` all dispatch. (`10 as ExitCode` is unaffected — an int→int cast keeps the value's `integer` tag.)
- **⚠ HARNESS ROBUSTNESS — A SPEC-TEST CHILD OUTLIVED ITS SUITE BY ~1h45m, AND TWO KNOWN FLAKES POINT AT THE SAME MECHANISM.** Filed 2026-07-25 (user: *"file it"*). ⚠⚠ **UPGRADED THE SAME DAY BY TWO MORE INCIDENTS — IT IS NOT ONE STRAY BINARY, IT IS A WHOLE RUN'S CHILD SET, AND IT PRODUCES A MISATTRIBUTABLE RED GATE.** (2) During the primitive-dispatch rung, **FOUR leaked `maxon-shv2` workers held `maxon-shv2.exe` open and made the next BUILD FAIL** — `error E9001: The process cannot access the file … because it is being used by another process` — **immediately after a rebase over ten upstream commits that touched the same two compiler files.** That is the dangerous part: the exit-1 looks exactly like a semantic rebase conflict, and the coordinator was one inference away from hunting a conflict that did not exist. Killing the four PIDs made the identical build succeed, exit 0, zero warnings. (3) Removing that worktree then failed on a leaked **`wasm-tools.exe`** from the same suite run. ⭐ **All five leaked processes carried the SAME start timestamp (01:06:40)** — four spec workers *and* the wasm toolchain helper — so a single green suite run leaked its entire child set, not a stray. ⇒ **A leaked child is not untidiness: it FAILS THE NEXT BUILD, MISATTRIBUTES the failure, and BLOCKS teardown for every concurrent rung.** The leak is also **intermittent** (a later identical battery leaked zero), which is the same profile as the sidecar flake below and further supports their sharing one mechanism. **THE HARNESS, NOT THE COMPILER** — so its gate is the suite plus a deliberate leak/handle check, not `/specs` coverage. **HARD EVIDENCE, measured:** PID **15964 `enum-array-push-count`**, started **2026-07-24 20:36:41**, was **still running at ~22:20** — holding an open handle on `specs-shv2/.spec-tmp/array-enum-element-size/enum-array-push-count.exe` (8,022 B). It **blocked `git worktree remove`** (`error: failed to delete … Invalid argument`) and `Remove-Item` (`Access to the path … is denied`); killing that one PID made the directory delete instantly. ⚠⚠ **The suite that spawned it reported 1581/0 — GREEN.** So either the runner collected the result and LEAKED the child, or the program did its work and hung on exit; **a test executable outliving its harness by hours is a defect either way, and the suite is structurally unable to notice.** **THREE SYMPTOMS, ONE PLAUSIBLE MECHANISM — the rung should test that hypothesis first rather than fixing them separately:** (1) this leaked child holding a handle **under `.spec-tmp`**; (2) the **fragment-SIDECAR flake** already noted in the P1.7a row — *"compile produced no sidecar"* on a **RANDOM unrelated** test, firing **even under `-j1`** (so not parallelism), where the "actual" is a **MISSING `.ir`** rather than different codegen, and every hit passes in isolation — which is exactly what a live handle or a not-yet-flushed file under `.spec-tmp` would produce; (3) **OPEN #48**, the pre-existing `.spec-tmp` race. **⇒ The unifying suspicion is child-process/handle lifetime around `.spec-tmp`, not filesystem timing** — and the P1.7a row's "harness note" and #48 fold into THIS rung rather than staying scattered. **What the rung owes:** a runner that **waits for and reaps every spawned child** (and fails loudly if one is still alive at suite end — a leaked child should be a RED suite, not an invisible orphan); a `.spec-tmp` lifetime rule stated once; and a check that a completed suite leaves **no live descendants and no open handles** under it. ⚠ Note the coordinator could not simply kill the blocker unattended, because a *different* agent's live worktree shares the repo — so a leaked child is not merely untidy, it **blocks worktree teardown for every concurrent rung**. **SEPARATE TOOLING BUG, same session, do not conflate:** the `maxon-dev` MCP `build` **spuriously refused** a valid worktree once with *"repoRoot … is not a Maxon checkout — it has no stdlib/ and maxon-sharp/"* and succeeded on immediate retry. It failed **CLOSED** (the correct direction, and by design), but a flaky refusal reads as a real misconfiguration to the next agent; it lives in `maxon-dev-mcp/mcp/`, whose binary is gitignored and needs an explicit rebuild + server restart.
- **◑ SCALE-CORPUS CATCH-UP — SLICE 1 DONE 2026-07-25, MERGED `29c06e7a9`** (implement `497c03341` · optimize `f4815babf` · review `29c06e7a9`; merged-tree x64 **1586/0**, wasm **1390/0**, `-j1`==`-j12` byte-identical, `specs-shv2/fragments` **EMPTY** — this rung is codegen-neutral by construction *except* for the optimizer's fix, which is codegen-neutral by measurement — `scale-test` exit 0, wall **14.7 s**, code growth x1.85–x1.99 vs the x1.50 floor; **rebased TWICE** over upstream, the second time after `merge --ff-only` correctly REFUSED a diverged base). **RED, measured by the coordinator before the wave: ZERO `match`/`enum`/`String`/`Array with`/`type … uses`/`interface`/`implements`/`print(`/`or`/`xor`/struct-field-WRITE in 109,697 lines of rung-5 source.** Ten knobs now generate them, each paired against the knob a future change must move independently of it (enum TYPES vs match SITES · String op sites vs managed-struct TYPES · trivial vs MANAGED Array groups · generic BASES vs INSTANTIATIONS · interface CONFORMANCES vs concrete DISPATCH SITES) plus a fixed-shape coverage file on `shiftCoverageSource`'s precedent. Counts verified by the coordinator at rung0/rung5: `match` 21/641, enums 6/130, `as String` 9/257, `.append` 16/512, `.push` 44/1408, generic `uses` 5/129, `interface` 8/132, field WRITE 41/1281; fixed coverage flat by design. Corpus rung0 19→30 files, rung5 143→185 files / 109,697→139,381 lines. **⭐⭐ IT PAID FOR ITSELF ON ITS FIRST RUN — the new heap knobs exposed a REAL SUPERLINEARITY the old ladder structurally could not see, and it was FIXED, not filed** (`f4815babf`): `regalloc:splitting` bytes ×192.1 over a ×128 ladder (**N^1.084**), per-rung ratio CLIMBING 1.77→2.61 while allocations stayed linear at ×2.03 — linear allocs + superlinear bytes = one O(N)-sized allocation made O(N) times. **TWO** scratch buffers in `SplitLiveRanges.maxon`, both sized by the FUNCTION and allocated per SPLIT: `materializePeak` took a fresh `wordsPerRow` live-before row on every peak (and `refreshPeak` materializes after EVERY split) ⇒ O(splits×values) bytes/function, now `SplitScratch.peakLiveBefore` grown with `growFilled` and zeroed in place; and `markBlocksReachableFromPeak` allocated its BFS worklist fresh per un-memoized (peak block, def block) pair, pushing a block per IN-EDGE ⇒ O(splits×edges), now `ReachCache.worklist`. Each saving QUADRUPLES per rung (1,568→48,474,016 and 832→104,249,328 bytes) — the signature of a splits×values term on a doubling ladder. **RESULT N^1.084 → N^0.972**, per-rung flat at 2.04, −41.9% of the phase at rung 7, −3.2% of the whole compile, every other phase and regalloc sub-phase bit-identical at all 8 rungs. ⚠ **A stale liveness row is a MISCOMPILE, not a crash**, so the pass's own `VerifyIncrementalSplit` was run (1581/0) and then SABOTAGED to prove it fires (11/15 `register-spill` red, `the peak's live-before row differs from a full sweep's at word 0`; 3 fail even with verify OFF); flag restored to `false`. ⭐⭐ **The independent review found a FALSE MANIFEST CLAIM — this file's signature bug, in the very file whose rule is *"a heading is a claim; an omission is a claim too"***: the new text claimed `Array with <T>` instances are "FIXED at four (one trivial, one String, one struct-element, one nested)" when there are **THREE and no nested array exists at any rung** (the coordinator's own count of 5 had been fooled by two occurrences inside a GENERATED COMMENT); the count is now derived from the generator's constants so the enumeration IS the count. It also caught `structTypesSource` still claiming "THERE IS NO `__destruct_*` CASCADE TO MEASURE YET" while citing a file that lists that lowering as performed — it shipped in P1.4a wave 2 and this rung's own manifest says so, i.e. the file contradicted itself. And it single-sourced a magic `2` written at both of `genericBasesSource`'s loops (`GenericBaseArityShapeCount`) plus two 2-way `if/else` shape selectors that would absorb a raised count in SILENCE (each `else` now panics) — emitted corpus byte-identical, structure only. **The host-panic exclusion is proved BY TEST, not by argument: rung0 compiles for `arm64-macos`, exit 0, Mach-O written** (coordinator-reproduced), and zero `where`/`Set`/opaque-`T` elements exist at any rung. The three `match` dispatch strategies were confirmed distinct in dumped IR (`x64.jumpTable`, a compare chain, a `cmp 200 / jcc less` binary-search pivot), so `EnumCasesPerType = 4` being `Parser.MinIntervalsForDispatch` is load-bearing. **`phase:insertRangeChecks` went INERT → ACTIVE** — the old corpus emitted no cast at all and only FULL-range aliases, which `rangeIsFull` refuses to guard, so the pass did *nothing* for the ladder's entire history; its x2.41 byte reading is a reallocation LUMP (ratio humps then FALLS BACK 2.41→2.26→2.07, which a quadratic cannot do) not a curve. **◑ SANCTIONED RESIDUALS (slice 2+, named up front, so this rung is NOT complete):** witness dispatch (`where T is I`), `Set`, and opaque-`T` managed array elements — all need **host-awareness in the runner** first (a decision: `compileRung` spawns `build` with no `--target`, so on arm64/wasm a witness call is a `panic()` that takes the whole run down as a BROKEN RUN); **closures** (arm64 genuinely unverified — this file contradicts itself: `#67` says `arm64EmitLeaFuncAddr` panics, a later line says the codegen landed but has no runner); **async**; **`for`-in**. **DESIGN CALL FOR THE USER (reviewer-surfaced, stdlib is out of an agent's bounds):** `stdlib/Array.maxon` has no geometric "refill that grows", so every caller wanting *reuse the storage, zero the whole row, extend* hand-rolls it — `zeroRow` is now that helper, locally; a `refillGrown` would be the real single source. **TOOLING BUG (not the compiler):** the `maxon-dev` MCP `build` spuriously refused once with *"repoRoot … is not a Maxon checkout — it has no stdlib/ and maxon-sharp/"* on a worktree that has both, succeeding on immediate retry. It failed CLOSED (the correct direction) but a flaky refusal reads as a real misconfiguration to the next agent. **Original rationale kept below, because it is why this was sequenced ahead of 2b-iii/2b-iv.**
- **⭐⭐ SCALE-CORPUS CATCH-UP — the RATIONALE (USER RULING 2026-07-24, sequenced ahead of P1.7a 2b-iii/2b-iv).** **The corpus measures the compiler roughly as it stood at P1.1a.** `Testing/ScaleCorpus.maxon:1601`'s own manifest declares under NOT GENERATED: *"`break` …; `or` / `xor` / `not`; print, arrays, strings, `for`, `match`; closures, async, generics; `as` casts"*, plus float ARGUMENTS/RETURNS, struct field WRITES and structs across a CALL. So every mechanism from P1.1b onward is **structurally invisible**: `match` (P1.1b), `String` (P1.2), closures + async (P1.5), generics (P1.6), `Array` (P1.7), interfaces/witnesses (P1.7a, under "generics"), `Set` (P1.7b). ⇒ **the recurring `scale allocsΔ0 … the corpus is generics-free / Set-free / conformance-free` lines in this very file are not evidence of anything** — a Δ0 for a corpus that cannot express the feature says only that the instrument is blind to it — and each optimizer instead rebuilds a THROWAWAY ladder to get a real answer (P1.7 3b-vi-b-α built a managed corpus because "scale-test's is managed-free"; P1.7a 2b-ii built a 64→2,048-operator `where T is Comparable` ladder, measured it linear, and discarded it). Nothing accumulates in the trend table, which is the whole deliverable. ⚠ **The gap is DECLARED, not hidden** — the *"an undeclared gap reads as coverage"* rule from the earlier corpus rung is working — so this rung EXTENDS coverage and is the opposite of touching the instrument to flatter a number. **Its plan must settle three things up front:** (1) **knob order** — generics/interfaces first (most compiler surface, most active rungs), then `match`, then `String`/`Array`; each knob carrying the per-knob rationale `ScaleCorpus.maxon` demands, and **every construct under exactly ONE manifest heading with a partly-generated one naming WHICH part**; (2) **trend continuity** — new knobs move every absolute number, so prior `docs/optimization-log.md` rows stop being comparable unless an explicit EPOCH boundary is recorded, or a second corpus profile keeps the scalar ladder's rows intact (the profile option doubles run time — decide, don't drift); (3) **scope honesty** — `scale-test` **COMPILES each rung and never RUNS it**, so these knobs trend the COMPILER's cost of compiling those constructs and **not** the emitted program's runtime, which remains measured by nothing and is its own instrument on its own rung. ⚠ Also remember the corpus's own warning that it **measures MEMORY only**, so a TIME-only superlinearity stays invisible here by design (2b-ii's optimizer hit exactly that with `requireInterfaceForParse` — see Workstream O's measured debt).
- **A COMPILER PANIC ON A VALID PROGRAM — an enum-typed LOCAL captured in a CLOSURE.** `let o = Color.green` then `let f = function(bump Small) gives bump + match o 'c' … end 'c'` ⇒ `panic at LowerMaxonToStd.maxon:49: maxonTypeToStdType: a 'named' type must be resolved to a primitive before lowering (TypeResolution did not run)`, in `MaxonToStdLowering.lowerOp`. **Pre-existing and NOT `Ordering`-specific: coordinator-reproduced on the pre-2b-ii base with a plain user `enum Color`**, and closure-capture code is untouched by every 2b-ii diff — so the capture path never gets the enum's `named` type resolved. An enum closure **parameter** works (runs 3), which localizes it to CAPTURE. Adjacent, same-shaped, also pre-existing and also confirmed user-enum-identical: `Array with <enum>` + `otherwise <Case>` ⇒ E3059, and a global initialized from a qualified enum case is unsupported. Found by 2b-ii's review while probing `Ordering` in unusual positions.
- **✅✅ #40 → CONDITIONAL OWNERSHIP OF A BORROWED MANAGED-AGGREGATE PARAM RE-BOUND TO A `var` — CLOSED 2026-07-23 (main `7057bf8ad` fix + `b9f13dcac` review, on `134600b69`; suite 1366/0, `--workers=1`==`12` byte-identical, no 101). RESOLUTION: user ruled approach (A) RETAIN-PROMOTION. `var q = <borrowed aggregate param>` now emits `__mm_retain(p)` (NEW `MmRuntime` fn returning the ptr — `__mm_incref` returns void, so a returning twin was needed) so `q` co-owns the box from declaration: owned on every path (then/else/break/continue/loop-carried), aliasing preserved (`q.x=100` still shows through, matching the oracle), refcount balances. A BOUNDED refcount exception like COW #105. The parser promotes via a unified `promoteBorrowedToOwned` (review-consolidated declaration + reassignment dispatch), mirroring `promoteMutableString` but incref-not-copy. The `borrowedToOwned` branch that `ownedBindings.push`ed at the wrong depth is thereby dead on this path. 13 oracle-derived tests in `specs-shv2/conditional-ownership-param.md`. scale-test: #40's ISOLATED footprint is +0 allocs / +8 bytes (the one `usesRetain` bool) — proven by a post-merge A/B vs the parent `134600b69` (bit-identical absolute allocs), the printed row delta being the upstream perf commit `ffc521235`. Kept below as the closed technical record.** **What #40 actually was** (established 2026-07-23 by a reachability probe + an implementer stop-and-report, after the coordinator first *wrongly* concluded it unreachable): a borrowed struct/union **PARAMETER** (a P1.4a pass-by-reference value — its owned-heap bit unset) re-bound to a `var` (`var q = p`) is a borrowed managed var that SKIPS String promotion, so a later `q = <owned>` hits `reassignBindingValue`'s `borrowedToOwned` branch (`Parser.maxon:~6153`), which `ownedBindings.push`es `q` at the CURRENT block depth. When the reassignment is in a NESTED block, `q`'s owned value drops at the inner block's exit while `q` (declared outer) lives on → **use-after-free**. CONFIRMED RED under poison-on-free (#108): struct nested → **`0x3F3F3F3F`** (1061109567), union nested → **`0xC0000005`** fault, and the **`break` / `continue` / loop-carried / used-after-loop** variants all UAF too; oracle gives the correct value (9 / 7). **Same-depth reassign is BENIGN** (push depth == decl depth → exit 9). ⚠ **THE NAIVE FIX (drop `q` at its declaration scope) IS WRONG — it ships a NEW else-path double-free** (implementer-proven from emitted IR): after the merge `q` is a phi `{borrowed p (else), owned Point(9) (then)}`; an unconditional drop at function exit frees the borrowed `p` on the else-path, and the CALLER also decrefs that param → double-free. No committed spec exercises the else-path-taken case, so the suite stays green over it. ⚠ **COPY-PROMOTION (String's fix) IS ALSO WRONG** — a struct `var q = p` ALIASES the param box (`q.x = 100` shows through to the caller's `p`; verified both compilers), so copying diverges from the oracle. (`var q = <owned LOCAL>` is a MOVE on shv2 / rejected E3078 on the bootstrap — so this is PARAM-specific.) ⇒ **THE DESIGN RULING THE RUNG MUST CARRY (every correct fix breaks a thesis invariant — decide at sequencing):** **(A) retain-promotion** — `incref` the borrowed aggregate param at `var q = p` so `q` co-owns the box from declaration (owned on every path, aliasing preserved, refcount balances); a BOUNDED refcount exception like COW #105; needs `__mm_retain(ptr) returns ptr` in `MmRuntime.maxon` (today's `__mm_incref` returns void) + parser promotion mirroring String; **expands the "escape is the only place refcounts appear" thesis.** **(B) parser-side drop-flags** — a runtime "owns?" bool through every merge + conditional decref; no refcount but ADDS runtime flags, which the model deliberately avoids ("reconcile at joins, no flags"); larger, higher double-free/leak risk. **(C) reject** — diverges from the oracle (scope cut). Coordinator recommends (A). **Poison-on-free (#108) is the standing detector — the RED anchors + the `mutate(Point)`/`pick(Num)` probes reproduce it today.** Reachable via P1.4a's borrowed aggregate params, so P1.4a-adjacent.
- **⭐ Array slice COW — SLICE A ✅ CLOSED 2026-07-22 (`12705a7d9` + review `66bd13c24`, 1162→1171, 1184/0 after rebase); SLICE B (zero-copy slices) IS THE REMAINING P1.7 RESIDUAL.** (Number TBD — the parallel repo files #NN concurrently, so assign at sequencing to avoid a collision.) *(User ruling 2026-07-22: "COW makes slices nearly free… worked in as early as possible, a residual if it can be implemented immediately".)* **SLICED A/B by the coordinator after the survey, because A fixes live crashes and is a strict prerequisite for B.**
  - **✅ SLICE A — DETACH-ON-WRITE.** `__arr_cow_detach(record)` gives a record a private writable buffer before any in-place write; the only non-owned buffer at this slice is the `b"…"` literal's immortal `.rdata` blob (`capacity<0`). **It fixed FIVE reachable segfaults on valid programs**, none of which any spec covered: `set`/`clear`/`remove`/`pop` on a literal wrote into read-only `.rdata` (`0xC0000005`, empty stderr), and **`resize(-2)` wrote BELOW the buffer** (`-2 >= -2` satisfied grow's check so nothing detached) — on an owned array that silently erased the `__mm_alloc` header and published `count() == -1`, so it is now a clean abort (exit **73**), grounded in `stdlib/Array.maxon:11`'s `resize(newLength ElementCount)` where `ElementCount = int(0 to u64.max)`: the contract was always non-negative, shv2 just was not asserting it. Expected values all taken from the RUNNABLE ORACLE. ⚠ **`/specs` has NO twin** — no corpus case mutates a byte-string literal, and `codegen-internals.md`'s `rdata-cow-*` family covers `let arr = [1,2,3]` constant arrays in `.rdata`, a route shv2 does not have — so the 9 cases are authored (precedent: the shv2-only `push-grows-off-rdata`), oracle-valued.
  - **⭐ THE CHECK LIVES IN THE EMITTED RUNTIME FUNCTION, NOT AT THE CALL SITES — a deliberate divergence from BOTH references, and the slice's thesis.** Both refs emit a COW check per mutation site and **both have holes because that list drifted**: the bootstrap's `clear` and `setLength` mutate a shared buffer with *no check at all* (`MaxonToStandardConversion.ManagedMemory.cs:1654`, `:1588`), and v1's `shift_left`/`shift_right` are saved only by an arithmetic accident. Putting the detach inside `__arr_*` makes "every mutation site is gated" a property of the runtime instead of a checklist. **Gating rule, stated once: a mutator either calls `__arr_grow` (whose FIRST op is an unconditional detach, ahead of its own `capacity >= required` early-out) or calls the detach itself.** ⇒ 5 call sites (`grow`/`set`/`clear`/`remove`/`pop`) gate all 9 mutators; a 6th in `insert` would be an unreachable branch. Verified adversarially via the INTERNED-TWIN test (mutate one `b"hey"`, read the other — rdata intact).
  - **✅ SLICE B — ZERO-COPY SLICES — CLOSED 2026-07-22** (`14a0aaa75` + review `cd77c6805`, 1184→**1199/0**). `.slice`/`.clone` return a VIEW: own 48-byte record, `buffer@0 = base + start·element_size` (**INTERIOR**), `capacity@16 = -1` (view sentinel, distinct from `-2` rdata), `parent@32` = the **BASE pointer `__mm_alloc` returned** (the base, not the parent RECORD — `__mm_decref` needs the header at `base-24`, and an interior pointer would read the parent's DATA). **`__mm_incref` returned**, installed by `installArrayRuntime` since the view is its only caller — the consumer `MmRuntime.maxon:362-380` had been waiting for. `__arr_cow_detach` gained **ARM 2** (an owner whose buffer's refcount is non-zero detaches too) exactly as slice A promised: another arm of the SAME test, **no call site touched**. `__arr_decref`/`__arr_grow` now `__mm_decref` the shared base instead of `__mm_free`. **Nested views FLATTEN** — a view's `parent@32` is always the ORIGINAL base, never another record, so there is no chain and nothing walks one. Managed elements stay E2015-rejected (see the trap below).
    **⭐ MEASURED, by the coordinator, not claimed:** 500 slices of a 200k array **469 ms → 15 ms**, and *doubling the slice size doubles the OLD time while the new stays flat* — O(n)→O(1), asymptotic not constant-factor. Optimizer confirmed depth-independence (2,000,000 slices at nesting depth 1/10/100/1000 → 121/97/107/98 ms; a chain walk would make depth 1000 ~1000× slower) and length-independence (~50 ns/slice, flat across 100×).
    **⭐ THE SABOTAGE THAT MATTERED:** deleting the incref turned **19 tests red** including `slice-outlives-its-parent` (exit **101**) — and exactly the three base-0 cases stayed green (`slice-of-empty-array-then-push`, `slice-of-byte-string-literal`, `clone-of-byte-string-literal`), which is correct: they owe no incref. The gate was seen RED before it was believed.
    ⚠⚠ **A SECOND SABOTAGE DID *NOT* GO RED, AND THAT IS THE MORE IMPORTANT FINDING** — see #108. Moving the buffer release *ahead* of the copy in `__arr_cow_detach` still passed 1197/0, because `__mm_free` reclaims nothing under the bump allocator, so the "freed" bytes read back intact. The spec prose claiming *"a release before the copy would read freed bytes"* was **FALSE AS WRITTEN** and was corrected: the ordering is a rule the function KEEPS, not one a gate PROTECTS. It becomes load-bearing the day the allocator reuses memory.
    ⚠ **`scale-test` MOVED and is attributed**: +78 allocs / +3,566 bytes / +24 `.text` bytes **FLAT at every rung** — proven by A/B (restoring the old threaded `emitRefcountCheckToLastOwner` shape returns every rung to the previous numbers EXACTLY) to be the 5 Std ops `emitAdjustRefcount` adds to `__mm_decref`'s `lower` block, at the one reachable call site per module. Bought to put `owners - 1` and the refcount RMW in ONE function each now that `__arr_cow_detach` reads that convention across a file boundary. Row written to `docs/optimization-log.md`.
  - ⚠⚠ **THE "REFERENCE TO PORT" PREMISE THIS ENTRY USED TO STATE IS MEASURED FALSE (survey 2026-07-22).** It said to port bootstrap `LowerManagedMemSlice`+`EmitCowCheck` and v1 `__managed_mem_slice`/`__managed_mem_cow_check`. **NEITHER reference has ever zero-copied a slice of an OWNED array.** v1's `__managed_mem_slice` (`stdlib/Internals.maxon:3818`) is an EAGER COPY (`parent = ROOT`, `capacity = sliceLen`) — its `capacity == -1` sentinel is *tested everywhere and produced nowhere*. The bootstrap views only IMMUTABLE sources (rdata `-2`, or another view `-1`) and **refuses** an owned one, in its own comment (`Strings.cs:1480`): *"heap-allocated source cannot be zero-copy because struct-level COW can't distinguish slice refs from normal refs."* Its parent-side detacher `maxon_cow_struct_detach` (`RuntimeEmitter.MemoryManager.cs:1402`) tests `refcount != 1`, is emitted into every binary, and is **CALLED FROM NOWHERE** — that dead function IS the "one-directional hole", and `docs/MEMORY_MANAGEMENT.md:474` documents it as if it were live. ⇒ **Slice B is a NEW DESIGN, not a port.**
  ⭐ **THE ONE DESIGN FORK a move-only compiler forces (settled for B by the survey):** a shared view must keep the parent's buffer ALIVE while it references it. **(A) REFCOUNT the shared buffer** — a bounded, deliberate exception to "no runtime refcount", ONLY for COW-shared array buffers. **(B) BORROW-LIVENESS (E3070)** — a compile-time-bounded borrow that provably can't outlive the parent; purer vs the thesis, but forbids slice-outlives-parent. ⇒ **pairs with the E3070 residual + the borrow-on-get thesis — one design thread.** **⇒ TAKE (A), ANCHORED ON THE BUFFER, NOT THE RECORD — and that is precisely why shv2 can do what neither reference could.** Both refs refcount the RECORD, where a count of 2 cannot distinguish *"two owners"* from *"someone views my buffer"* — exactly why the bootstrap gave up. **shv2's array buffer is a SEPARATE `__mm_alloc` allocation whose own refcount slot at `buffer-8` is currently always 0 and read by nothing** (`MmRuntime.maxon:48`, `ArrayRuntime.maxon:744-746`), so a non-zero count there means one thing only: someone is viewing these bytes. shv2 gets that unambiguous signal **because** it is move-only. `__mm_incref` returns here, which is the caller `MmRuntime.maxon:362-380` explicitly anticipates. ⚠ Keep the E2015 rejection of MANAGED slice/clone/append: v1's `cow_check` does **no element retain** and is sound only because its two reachable triggers stamp `element_destroy = 0` — a managed-element view detaching shallowly is a double-free (v1 `Internals.maxon:3843-3849` names the UAF it already paid for).
- **#103 — builtin array methods do not enforce their RANGED parameter types, so every bounds guard is hand-written.**
  `Parser.parseArrayOneArg` types a builtin array method's argument as plain `integer`, so `stdlib/Array.maxon`'s
  `ElementIndex` / `ElementCount = int(0 to u64.max)` are not applied at the call site. That is *why* `get`/`set`/
  `remove`/`insert`/`slice` each carry a runtime `emitIsNegative` guard and why `resize` needed a new one (#102,
  abort 73) — shv2's `StdCmpPred` has no unsigned compare, so a negative slips every signed `>=` test. **A
  compile-time rejection at the call site would SUBSUME all of them** and turn a class of runtime aborts into
  E3005s. Front-end (parser + P1.9's range machinery), orthogonal to the array runtime. *(Surfaced by the COW
  slice-A rung; it is the generalization of the five segfaults that rung fixed one at a time.)*
- **✅ #104 — runtime ABORT exit codes now have ONE home, and the duplicate check IS THE LANGUAGE — CLOSED 2026-07-22** (main `0be8c7465`). The whole space became a single `RuntimeAbort` `enum`, so two cases sharing a raw value is `E3030`/`E3031` **from the bootstrap that compiles shv2** — no hand-written checker to rot, verified RED before relying on it. The 70/70 collision (`GtRuntime.ProcStoreOverflowExitCode` vs `ArrayRuntime.ElementSizeZeroExitCode`) was resolved by moving the array's unreachable `elementSizeZero` 70→74 (GT's 70 is spec-pinned); `101` (leak gate) and `134` (`userPanic`) folded in as reserved cases. `emitVoidAbort`'s `osExit`+dead-terminator fact single-homed as `emitRuntimeAbort`, which **derives the terminator from `func.returnType`** (so a caller cannot pick the wrong one), folding in `GtRuntime`'s two open-codings AND `buildArrCreate`'s value-returning twin (a prior review's "keep it separate" finding overturned on its own terms — it was conditioned on being the file's only value-returning abort, and `__gt_process_run` is a second). ⚠ Residual: `SpecWorkerPool.LeakCheckExitCode = 101` stays spelled locally BY DESIGN — a worker is the shv2 binary compiled by the C# bootstrap, so its 101 is `maxon-sharp`'s leak gate, not this enum's; the review corrected the note that framed it as an unresolved copy.
- **✅ #105 — ONE buffer-ownership encoding for String and Array, made STRUCTURAL — CLOSED 2026-07-22** (main `0be8c7465`). New `Compiler/Runtime/BufferOwnership.maxon`: **`capacity@16 < 0` ⇒ "not mine to write or release"** (the negative records only provenance — `ViewBufferCapacity -1`, `RdataBufferCapacity -2`, `InlineBufferCapacity -3`); **`parent@32` ⇒ "which separate allocation do I owe a `__mm_decref`"** or `NoOwedAllocation` (0). Three exported predicates read by BOTH runtimes; String's `DetachedRootParent`/`InlineParentSentinel` and both rdata sentinels DELETED (a detached String is simply an owner; an inline String a negative-capacity non-owner, so `emitStringInterp` was changed and `InlineParentSentinel` — written by the parser, read by nothing — is gone, 150 interpolation goldens −1 op each). ⭐ **Made STRUCTURAL not commented:** the subsumption `__str_append` relies on (`capacity < requiredLen` subsumes the sign) is enforced by `typealias NotOwnedBufferCapacity = int(i64.min to -1)` — a fourth provenance written `= 0` **does not compile** (verified E3005). ⭐ **This also fixed [[#43]]** — see the P1.2 rung: `__str_append` released the old buffer BEFORE the blit read it (`s.append(s)` read-after-release), proven a real UAF with a poisoning `__mm_free`.
- **✅ #107 — `try` binds the OUTERMOST call of a postfix chain — CLOSED 2026-07-22** (main `83d072d35`, →1308/0). Two bugs in one mechanism: (a) `parseTryCallTarget` stopped at the primary (`make()`), leaving `.slice(…)` unconsumed, so the tail-rewrite converted the wrong call — fixed by running the full postfix loop (`await` stays terminal), matching the bootstrap's `ParsePrimary`+`RewriteTailCallAsTryCall`; (b) E3055's gate asked `calleeIsDeclared`, **false** for compiler-internal callees, so `try arr.count()` COMPILED and desugared to branch on an error flag the callee never writes — fixed to read `isThrowingArrayRuntimeCallee` (the same predicate `SemanticCheck` reads for the mirror E3057). ⭐ **The fix cascaded FOUR more reachable defects the suite was green over, all found by probing and all FIXED, not filed:** (1) **the `pendingTempDrops` NESTING bug** — a flat list with no discipline meant a branching construct's condition/scrutinee/arm temporaries were drained by the wrong block: on too few paths ⇒ **leak** (`if`/`else if`/`while`/`match`-scrutinee, exit 101), on a path that never built the value ⇒ **register-allocator PANIC** (`match`-expr arm / ternary arm / short-circuit RHS — a *compiler crash on a valid program*). Cured generally: each forking region marks its pending height and drops what it built while control is single-threaded. (2) **array-slots UAF** — `try arr.get(i) otherwise <owned box>` merged a borrow (ok) with an owned fallback (error), dropping the fallback before `result.value` read it; fixed by increfing the ok-edge borrow so the phi is uniformly owned. (3) **aliased-binding DOUBLE-FREE** — `try … otherwise <live binding>` aliased the binding on the error edge with no incref, so scope-exit dropped one box from two owners (exit 101, reached the owned-result arm and non-array programs too); fixed by `transferFallbackToPhi` — MOVE an unconsumed temp, INCREF a live binding, the discriminator being `pendingTempsContain`. **⚠ (3) had been deferred by a review on the false premise "rejected by the type checker anyway"; the coordinator tested the deferred shape, found it a masked double-free, and sent it back — the deferral, not just the fix, needed verifying.** `__mm_incref`'s install moved `usesArray`→`usesIncref` (the `try` phi is now a non-array caller). *(Found by the COW slice-B rung.)*
- **✅ #108 — `__mm_free` POISONS the freed payload, ALWAYS ON — CLOSED 2026-07-23** (main `145a42fa8`). Not a flag (user directive: "always be on"): `__mm_free` unconditionally overwrites `[ptr, ptr+size)` with `PoisonByte = 0x3F` the instant a box dies, so the bump allocator's never-reused-slot no longer hides a read-after-free — it now returns 63, or faults on the non-canonical poisoned pointer `0x3F3F3F3F3F3F3F3F`, instead of the stale-but-correct bytes. The size comes from the header (`HeaderSizeOffset`), so the range is exactly the payload. **The whole class this was filed for is now a STANDING GATE**: the committed suite runs under poison on every spec run. **PROVEN TO HAVE TEETH, not merely green** (a gate is not evidence until seen RED): moving `__str_append`'s release ahead of its blit (the #43 bug) made `var s="abc"; s.append(s); s.append(s)` print `abcabc??????` under poison, restored → `abcabcabcabc`; the review independently re-proved RED with a different `__str_decref` sabotage (`0xC0000005` fault). **Suite 1308/0 under poison** — no committed test masks a UAF the suite reaches. GOLDEN-NEUTRAL (`__mm_free` is `__`-prefixed, excluded from fragments; ZERO churn). scale-test row recorded: +352 allocs / +15,442 bytes FLAT per rung — the COMPILER compiling the now-larger `__mm_free` once per module, a per-module constant, not the emitted program's poison cost (which scale-test structurally cannot see). Review single-sourced the byte-fill loop the poison introduced (a near-copy of `ArrayRuntime.emitZeroRange`) into `MmRuntime.emitFillRange(dest, byteCount, fillValue)`, homed in the base layer, with the four array-zeroing sites now sharing it (`emitZeroRange` deleted, net −14 lines). ⚠ **Pairs with the zeroing contract — this is why it landed NOW, before a recycling free list:** once `__mm_free` returns a slot to a list, the reused slot must be re-zeroed on the way out of `__mm_alloc` (poison then zero) or the `[length, capacity)`-reads-zero invariant breaks. ⇒ **The residuals it now detects the day they become suite-reachable: P1.2 #40 (nested borrowed→owned depth-model) and the P1.7 borrow-on-get residual.** *(This began as a throwaway ~20–30-line instrument during the #43/#105/#107 batch — it confirmed #43 was a real read-after-release, found the array-slots UAF #107 fixed, and distinguished a masked double-free from a leak, which is a distinction only poison can draw. Now permanent.)*
- **#26 — function overloading.** `commitFuncSignatures` keys duplicate-function detection by NAME alone
  → E3006, so two same-name arities (`Counter.create` arity 0 + 2) collide; no `overload` resolution exists.
  Blocks `structs.md` `struct-field-default`, and the stdlib's overload sets (§487/§817) lean on it. Function
  resolution, orthogonal to structs.
- **✅ #65 — implicit-self method resolution — CLOSED 2026-07-23** (main `55cafda11`, 1335→1347/0). A bare
  call `foo(args)` inside a method body now resolves to the sibling method `foo` and routes through the SAME
  `parseCallNamed`/`ReceiverArg` path as explicit `self.foo()` (byte-identical IR, zero fragment M). ⭐ **RULE
  (oracle-verified): prepend `self` IFF the resolved sibling TAKES A RECEIVER** — an instance sibling gets
  self, a STATIC sibling gets none; a bare call in a static method has no self to prepend; a method WINS over a
  same-named free function; static→instance falls out as a clean E3036 (arity). `ensureSiblingReceivers` is a
  lazy per-type token walk (the canonical `funcSignatures` isn't assembled at real-parse time, and the
  file-local artifact holds only backward-declared methods — a forward-referenced sibling can't be queried
  otherwise). The implementer caught + fixed a crash IT introduced (a generic `sizeof(T)`-reading sibling via a
  bare call panicked in `forwardCallerLayout` — the descriptor-need scan only saw literal `self.method()`).
  Review found + fixed TWO duplication defects: (1a) the bare receiver-resolution had a silent receiver-less
  fallback where the explicit path refuses self-capture, so a bare sibling call inside a self-capturing closure
  gave E3036 where `self.foo()` gives the P1.5 E2015 — un-forked via a shared `requireNotInSelfCapture`; (1b)
  the "is this `static`?" token test was written a THIRD time — shared `functionDeclIsStatic`. `throw-transfers-
  ownership.md`'s `propagate-throw-through-local-struct` after #65 blocked ONLY on #64 (borrowed-self throw),
